small bag of potatoes. Hefting me up into his thick arms, he doesn’t sling my over his
shoulder this time; which is a surprise. Instead, he carries me close to his shoulder, like one
would a child. He’s that incredibly strong that he only needs one arm to hold me up.

Up close now, I hold my breath and stare at his face, which is now within inches of mine. His
sneer is one of impatience, causing the various puckers scars across his skin to move and
tighten. The one across his whitened eye, in particular, looks the nastiest. I stiffen, unable to
look away from it. His breath hits my face but it doesn’t smell unpleasant. In fact,
considering how beastly he looked, he smelt pretty decent. Like moistened earth and
something wild. Something definitely male.

I realise belatedly that his expression has softened. He’s observing me just as closely as I am
him. His eyes, strange and mismatched, cruise over my features and hair with keen interest.

I must have made a sound, a soft gasp, as he suddenly snaps back to focus and grunts at me.
His arm tightens, stilling my squirms. The thick length of his tail joins against my waist,
wrapping against me there like a heavy python.



“Foolish creature,” he growls, his clicks fast and low, vibrating straight through me. “For all
your smallness you have no sense of stealth. It’s no wonder your mate does not come for you,
you make too much noise.”

I open my mouth, about to snap something back, but then freeze when we both hear
something crack from within the shadows of the trees beside us. Darvik’s head whips to it, as
does mine. I clutch at his shoulder as a sudden sense of being watched follows. My skin
prickles as all my senses careen together, trying to tell me something. 

“What was that?” I whisper, more to myself than to him.

He growls lowly, ignoring my question. Clutching me closer, he starts to carry me with
swiftness further through the foliage. Leaves and branches whip as us as we go and all I can
do it hold onto him. In a way, I was kind grateful for the fact he was carrying me; at least my
legs were able to rest after all the walking we’d done most the morning.

For what seems like an hour or so, he leaps through the undergrowth, pausing and sniffing at
the air occasionally like a dog on the hunt. At one point, he pauses near a bush, picking a
handful of berries and passing them to me, all the while scanning his surroundings and
starting up again in his hurried pace. I chew on each one, cringing at their tartness. At least it
was something, I thought. The fact he’d even bothered to stop to feed me was a surprise in
itself. 

Growing tired and somewhat bored, I lean into him, resting my head against his shoulder. His
muscular body is as taut as bowstring against me, hard and unforgiving as stone. The
thickness of his dreadlock-like hair skims against my knuckles as he turns to peer into the
trees. His skin is slick, somewhat oily and as dark as burnished copper. Without thought, my
fingers skim over a roughened patch that lines over the slope of his collarbone. The flesh
there is textured yet soft like leather, reminding me of the scales of a snake.

At my touch, he staggers in his step, drawing to an abrupt halt. I sit upright and snap my hand
back, throwing him an apologetic yet sheepish wince.

“Sorry, my bad.”

The colours of his eyes are startling, burning right into me like two flames; fire and ice
combined. The tusks of his mandibles clatter together as he lifts a mottled brow at me. A
head tilt follows. He seems almost… curious. 

Once again his focus falls to my hair and I try not to flinch as his free hand comes up to reach
for it. He clumsily grabs for a lock, dragging it gently out to feel it against his thickened
fingertips. Despite having his razor-sharp claws so close to my skin, he’s actually being very
gentle about it.

“Taz’arin,” he rumbles it in a near whisper.

I frown and swallow thickly, feeling myself stiffen like a board against him.

“What does that mea--?” I don’t get to finish my question.



Something cracks again from nearby, only louder and closer this time. I catch the hint of
shapes from within the shadows to our left. My heart leaps as the foliage their rustles, slowly
parting. Something silvery glints and reflects the sunlight directly into my eyes, blinding me
for a moment.

A chorus of low threatening growls all but swamp around us like the rolling of thunder. The
things that had been following us finally stalk forward and reveal themselves. Three massive
male figures stride closer, claws clacking, muscled chests flexing in preparation for the
coming fight. Three four-legged companions follow at their heels, snarling and snapping with
vicious intent.

I recognise them instantly and my hope surges, bringing a massive grin to my face.

“Chakria.” One of them purrs to me and I feel a desperate shudder wrack down my spine.

They’re here. It’s all I can think as they draw to silent stop, forming a semi-circle around us. 

Memnis’ jaws splay wide in aggression as he takes up position to our right. For an instant, his
glowing yellow eyes cast to me in obvious concern. Yaknaur drops to a crouch to our left, the
blades in his hands gleaming like silver. Sin’Chila comes up between them, pausing his giant
self a short distance away, his expression is icy and yet hungry for violence.

He reaches down and pulls out his spear. With a swift motion, he slams it to the ground,
causing it to lengthen and crackle with blue-ish thrumming light. His entire being expands
before my very eyes with growing threat.

His lethal eyes slide to me, burning bright, before sliding back to his new enemy. Darvik.

“Give us the female,” he hisses with a snap of his jaws, “and we will make your passing a
swift and painless one.”



Chapter 31

They’ll kill him.

It’s the first thing that springs to mind as I sit there perched within Darvik’s arms, watching
as my hunter mates prowl forward with bloodthirst and violence in their eyes. My gaze slides
over each of them in turn. First to Yaknaur, who stands strong and steady, visibly preparing
for the fight that would inevitably come. Then to Sin’Chila, who leads the trio with his
weapon at the ready and a cunning gleam in the depths of his sunken eyes. They trail over
their new adversary, weighing him up, evaluating his strength and weaknesses. Finally, I look
to Memnis, who is quietly fixed on me. Although he does not speak, I can sense his unease.
His frown is troubled as his mandibles flex and flare with restlessness. I try and flash him a
reassuring smile and he nods back slowly but it doesn’t seem to settle him at all. If anything,
I notice his claws flexing as he circles further around to the side of us, attempting to use the
shadows there to slip into Darvik’s blind spot.

“This is your last chance,” Sin’Chila warns, baring the inside of his fleshy mouth. He draws
the tip of his spear higher, pointing it towards us with clear intent. “The female. Now… or
your head… make a choice!”

Out of nervousness, my grip on Darvik’s shoulder tightens. In response, his embrace firms
and his tail all but chains me to his torso. Its long length is thick and heavy, nearly
constricting the very air from my lungs. Something sharp digs into my thigh; his claws. They
don’t pierce the skin but its enough to make me very aware of where they are on my person.

“Darvik…” I lick my lips anxiously, never taking my gaze from Sin’Chila who is waiting for
the other hunter’s response with barely contained impatience. “You must listen to me,” I
whisper to him. “They mean what they say. You have to--”

“I will give you nothing,” Darvik growls back, cutting me off. He slinks back, putting some
distance between us and the three males, all the while clutching me harder to him, unwilling
to relinquish me to them.

Memnis and Yaknaur move into the corner of my vision, creeping closer. Whether Darvik
knows of their approach, I didn’t know, but I certainly did. My skin crawls uneasily as they
prowl nearer, the tension becoming absolute torture. I want nothing more than to shove away
from my captor and run to them, yet at the same time, I could already sense that it was too
late to prevent any violence from occurring. All it would take is one wrong move and all of
hell would be unleashed. These males were out for blood and I only hoped that I wouldn’t get
caught in it. Darvik was making it very clear that he had no intention of letting me go or even
putting me down.

With my dread climbing, I glance to Memnis, beseeching to him with my eyes alone.

I don’t know what to do. I’m so on edge that I can scarcely draw breath. My entire frame
locks. Waiting.



A long moment passes, horribly tight, thick with so much fury and hatred that I can almost
taste it.

“I answer to no one.” It rumbles through me as Darvik retorts. And then, as if to make things
a million times worse, he says to them. “Least of all to weakling Ser’al’ak like you.”

I have no idea of what he’d just called them, but going by Yaknaur’s swiftly darkening
expression, it wasn’t anything polite.

“Don’t!” I shout and start to wriggle my way free. I had to do something. I had to at least try
and calm them all down before someone got hurt. “Please, don’t fight! This isn’t—Ugh!”

The tail around my midriff constricts and I choke on my words. If at all possible, Darvik is
squeezing me for dear life, nearly to the point of pain. Just when I think he’s about to snap at
me for interfering, his face turns into my hair, burrowing there. I stiffen, even as a chorus of
displeased hisses rise up around us.

I suck in air and then gasp when I realise he’s not hurting me at all. In fact, his behaviour is
bordering on the brink between being possessive and protective. His jaws expand and comb
through my hair in a common gesture of comfort. Coming from him, the behaviour is strange
and foreign. His trill is low as if sensing my fear and wanting to soothe me.

“Be easy, Taz’arin.” There it was again; that nickname he keeps calling me. His breath
breezes against my hair and cheeks, causing my eyelashes to flutter at his closeness. “I will
not allow them to harm you.”

His words both surprise and unsettle me.

“They’re not trying to hurt me!” I spin to him and push against his chest. “I know these
males. Please! You need to put me down. They’ll kill you if you don’t!”

Could he not see how dangerous this situation was going to get? The fact that Sin’Chila was
even allowing him a chance to peacefully let me go was astounding, to say the least. If
Darvik hesitated for much longer, I doubted very much that he’d continue to allow such a
small mercy.

“Hush… Fear not.” Darvik says, not yet looking away from the foe now gathering around
him. “I am stronger than you think. I will not lose. I never lose...I can take them.”

What? His arrogance is staggering, to the point where I almost laugh. Was he being serious?
Big as he may be, there were three Daku hounds and three very pissed off Yautja hunters to
contend with. He couldn’t win this fight; it was impossible. Madness, even.

He completely ignores my pleas and takes a moment to snarl at a hound who has wandered
too close to his left. The four-legged beast emits a hiss, its long tongue sliding out to taste the
air. The quills on its back ripple as it hunkers down, preparing to charge.

Darvik snaps out his free arm and two long blades dispatch from his wrist gauntlet;
reminding me of that character from the Xmen movies – Wolverine. Their serrated edges



gleam as he holds them up in front of us, his meaning clear; come any closer and you’ll get
your face shredded.

What he says next stuns us all.

“The hooman is mine.” The entire world around us seems to hold its breath in terror. “And
the only way you will have her is by taking her from my cold and rotting corpse.”

Oh god... Not good. That was definitely a very wrong thing to say.

Sin’Chila, with his gaze blazing, simply nods. “Very well. As you wish.”

And then it begins...

I blink – its a mere scant second of a moment – and then the entire mass of bodies around me
spring into action. Chaos ensues as Yaknaur roars, vaulting forward from the side. Sin’Chila
follows, joining his brother in his attack. I yelp as I’m all but tossed from one arm to another
like a sack of grain, lugging me out of reach as they attempt to grab for me. Everything spins
in a kaleidoscope of colour and shapes. They rush forward again and I’m wrenched away, a
twin set of blades slashing out when they draw too close. I gasp when a strike catches
Yaknaur across his massive chest, slicing it neatly until the flesh splits and his yellowish
blood drips down his torso.

“No! Stop it!”

They don’t hear me. None of them do. It’s too late for that.

A hound leaps at us and Darvik’s tail whips out and knocks it back like one would a tennis
ball. It slams into a trunk, spine first, before slumping with a winded howl.

Weapon drawn at the front, tail covering his back, Darvik manages to calculate each attack
and prevent it from coming. He boots back another beast like its nothing more than a football,
only to then spin on his heel, ready for when Memnis tries to vault at him from the shadows.
He catches my hunter mid-jump, grabbing him by his chest armour before slinging him away
like he’s merely a rag doll. All the while, he’s holding me against his side like I’m his
greatest treasure. He doesn’t drop me, not even once. Even scarier still was the fact that he
was managing to fight them all off with only one arm to defend himself with.

A set of star-shaped throwing knives come our way out of nowhere. With a hum, they circle
and slice through the air, nearly skimming right by my face as Darvik rotates at the last
possible moment. I gasp again, somewhat in relief, gripping onto his shoulder for dear life.

Wow! That had been close. Too close. Had Darvik not moved quickly enough I would have
been hit by them; a thought that downright crippled me with horror.

“Careful, Brothers!” Sin’Chila roars from somewhere nearby. I can’t see him due to my face
being pressed into a firm, warm chest. “Watch your aim! Do not hit the female. He carries her
too closely!”



Sin’Chila is suddenly there, twirling his spear with expertise, bringing its length down with
all the might of a titan. Darvik drops, turning to cover me protectively. He takes the strike
upon his back without so much as a flinch. At the impact, wood cracks and the staff splinters
loudly into two. Taking Sin’Chila’s moment of surprise, Darvik jumps up, carrying me with
ease, whilst landing a sound roundhouse kick to his opponents head. It happens so fast I can
barely keep track of it all. The force of it sends him flying back to the ground with an oomph.

Holy mother of…

There’s no time to react as Yaknaur dives in to defend his brother. For an instant, Darvik lets
me go to grapple with him and I feel myself start to fall. My stomach plummets as I claw to
keep myself over his shoulder. The last thing I want is to drop down between the two of them
when they’re trying to render each other to pieces.

Like two bulls, the two males strain and push against one another. A stampede of muscle.
Claws slash and heads slams together in a battle of strength against strength.

Sweat gleams across Darvik’s back as I struggle to hold on, even using my legs to cling to
him like a sloth. Their conjoined roars are savage, not in the slightest bit human.

No matter how much they try Darvik is keeping them at bay every time. How was he even
managing to do it? I was beyond perplexed (and somewhat quietly impressed). Despite the
fact that there was three of them, Darvik was easily winning the fight. Like a sturdy tree, he
did not move or retreat. He stood fierce in the face of his adversaries, towering over them all,
including Yaknaur; which was saying something.

I continue to shout for them to stop, my voice drowned out by their violence. Jaws snap and
someone behind me bellows out in pain.

And then I see him…

Memnis.

He’s slinking up behind Darvik, as stealthy as a panther. His slender black form makes not
even the slightest noise as he approaches, almost within reach. To my surprise, he doesn’t go
for Darvik. Instead, he locks his gaze with mine. My stomach knots. My keeper's attention is
on the foe in front of him, completely oblivious to the one coming from behind. His tail
sways to one side and Memnis merely sidesteps it and creeps nearer.

He lifts his arms to me, his soundless command clear.

Come.

This was it. This was my chance to go to him and get out of the way of the fight. Heart
pounding, I squirm higher over Darvik’s shoulder, pressing my knee there as I bend over his
back, reaching for Memnis’ hands.

I strain, arm shaking as our fingertip scarcely brush together in brief contact. Almost there.

“No!”



Suddenly, Darvik stiffens and I sense his head turning as he realises what’s happening. He
lands a hard kick into Yaknaur’s sternum, sending the huge male back and tumbling over a
fallen tree. He yanks me away from Memnis, spinning to bark in his direction.

“Get back!”

Their hisses are guttural and deafening.

Something sings through the air, whizzing past Darvik’s shoulder. It skims him there and in a
clumsy error, he loses his grip and I fall to the ground, landing hard upon my knees. In haste,
I start to crawl away.

Something latches itself around my ankle. His tail. Twisting, I gasp as he drags me back to
him.

My mind is in panic. My breathing is laboured and frantic.

“Memnis!” I call out to him with my hand still outstretched out even as the distance between
us grows again.

My cry echoes. The name sounding like the cracking of a whip, loud and shrill.

Everything seems to freeze then, even the wind seizes to blow.

Breathing hard, the hunter over me stills and slowly tilts his head to look down at me…

 

 

Darvik's POV

 

Memnisk.

This name. He knew this name. He’d heard it before, Darvik was sure of it.

The female below him squirms, trying to free herself from his grasp, fighting him. Could she
not see he was trying to protect her? These hunters were the ones who had been following
them, of that he was certain. They had hunted them and perhaps meant to harm her. So then,
why was she behaving so foolishly?

“Enough! Female!” He grabs her by the nape and shakes her a little; she must see reason.
“Bet still!”

“Memnis!”



Again, she shouts to the other hunter. It is almost as though she knows him, and he her. He, in
turn, shifts closer, as if tempted to go to her. However, the small ones courage fades at the
warning rumbles now rising from Darvik’s throat. A challenge. A promise.

Scowling, Darvik hunkers over the hoomans tiny frame, sheltering her under his larger one. If
they sought to take the female for their own then they would have to get through him; he
would not accept defeat.

He lifts his head and glares at the smaller male again. Determined, yet wary. This one is
quick and cunning, but he is also not showing any sign of attacking.

“Lar-tee…” The hunter speaks, the sound low, like a whine. His attention is fixed on the
female, only briefly rising to Darvik’s before dropping back to her. Torn. Uncertain.

Lor-tree? What was this foreign word? It was not the tongue of his people.

Come. Darvik urges in silent thought, leaning further over the hooman possessively. Come
and try to take her from me.

This one was shorter than the other two; an easy opponent.

Whatever he sees in Darvik’s expression forces him to retreat a step. Slowly, the male drops
to his knees bringing himself to their level. His mandibles flare, though not in aggression. He
trills in a low pleading tone, like a pup whining for a scrap of meat.

In response, Darvik feels the female shifting under him again. Looking down, he stares,
watching as she tries to scurry away again. With careful prey-like movements, she heads
towards the unknown hunter.

Frowning deepening, Darvik reaches for her leg again and pulls her back before she can get
too far away.

Foolish creature. What was she--

“Darvik, no!” A strange noise emits from her throat; a whimpering cry like that of a wounded
animal. “Please. Let me go…” she rasps, breathing harshly. “Let me go to him.”

Go to him? Confusion growing, he simply stares at her, his anger churning. Did she have a
death wish? Did she fear him that much that she was willing to throw herself to the mercy of
those who only wished to kill her? His fury boils as his chest expands, pride wounded.

With a bark, he starts towards her again, only to pause. Hovering. Watching. Curiosity rears
its head.

Taking the opportunity, the female practically throws herself forward, closing the space and
falling into the waiting outstretched arms of the male that wasn’t him.

Surprise slams into him as he watches the strange exchange that follows. The male does not
attack the hooman, nor does he push her away when the female wraps her slender arms about
his neck, burrowing into his mane.



The cries she had been emitting grow in volume, becoming frantically loud as she holds onto
him as though he is her very life force.

“You came.” He hears the whisper. “You finally came.”

To his further astonishment, the younger male wraps her in his arms and nuzzles into her,
purring soothingly like one would… to a mate.

Memnisk.

Ah, yes. It hits him then. He eyes them closely, observing the pair with part disgust and
intrigue. Was her mate then? The one she had been waiting for all this time?

Darvik snorts, shaking his head. But this male was a Yautja? Was he not? One of his own,
even though she was a hooman.

The second male – the biggest of three – strides forward and kneels beside them, huddling
close to them both. The female reaches for him too, turning into him as she had the other one,
rubbing and seeking his warmth.

“Yaknaur.” Her alien mouth presses to the side of the new males face as wetness seeps from
her eyes. Surrendering, she submits to their touches, welcoming them.

So many emotions bombard him. First hate, followed by confusion and then a hollow
emptiness that echoes painfully within his breast. All of the bloodlust he’d freed drains away
in an instant; the female has deserted him and there is further need to fight. The defeat is his.
This female has decided his fate… and she has chosen them.

Chakria, they called her. It was an ancient phrase, one hardly used by his people any more. In
times of old, it would symbolise the loyalty between mates, the most unbreakable of bonds. A
chosen female. For life.

Before, he had dismissed the scar-like mark upon the female's arm. And now, too late, he
realised that it was the same as the one on the trios armour. The symbol of their clan.

The truth of it wounds him more than he’d like to admit as it dawns on him. She did not have
just one mate, as he’d originally thought but three of them. They were all her males, and the
third was--

A large shape settles at his side, towering there and casting a shadow over his knelt form.
Bleakly, he lifts his head, already knowing who it is. The third member of their brotherhood
stands there and their eyes clash in meeting before the sharpened edge of a spear is placed at
Darvik’s throat.

With no fire left in his veins, Darvik simply stays there and stares up at their leader, waiting
for the killing strike that would inevitably come.

“I gave you fair warning, warrior,” the large male sneers down at him, digging the tip deeper
into his throat, drawing blood. “And instead you chose death. So now… you must die.”



Darvik blinks slowly and nods, jutting his chin, ready to face his end with courage. The spear
rises, readying to impale his flesh. The arm holding it brings it down and--

“Stop!” A female voice cries, shattering the stillness. “No! Sin’Chila, don’t!”

 



Chapter 32

“Please, don’t hurt him!” I shout, diving forward to grab for Sin’Chila’s bulging bicep. I tug
on him, drawing his focus away from Darvik and down to me. His face is like thunder,
furious at being interrupted.

“You…” he inhales deeply, seeking the last remaining shred of his patience. “You wish for us
to spare him? After all that he has done?” He points his spear towards the kneeling hunter in
disdain.

I shake my head, insistent. “He has never harmed me, Sin’Chila. In fact, I owe him a lot,
more than you realise..”

It wasn’t exactly a lie, per se. It was true that – albeit begrudgingly -Darvik had kept me safe
since returning to this planet. Without him, I would have probably died or been eaten by
some wild creature. I mean, sure, the guy could be a bit of a grump sometimes, but he could
also be decent. He’d fed me and kept me safe; even if most of the time it had been for his
own benefit. At any time, he could have killed me or left me to die. Any other hunter would
have probably taken my skin and used it to make a coat with.

Something about him drew me to him. On some level, over the last few days, I’d managed to
connect with him, perhaps even bonded to him – though I would never admit that to anyone
else.

“If it weren’t for him I would still be on that ship, being dragged back to Earth and unable to
see you again,” I reason.

Sin’Chila chuckles dryly. “Never. We would have found you…” He lifts a hand, being careful
to bend his claws away as he strokes the curve of my cheek. “Do you doubt the abilities of
your mates, Chakria?”

I grab for his large fingers and squeeze. “Of course not. Look, I know you’re angry because
he took me, but can’t you see? In a way, he’s done us all a favour by doing it. Now I’m here
and we’ve found each other again.”

Memnis and Yakanur stand to one side, observing our exchange with interest.

Sin’Chila grunts and pulls away, unmoved. “Not good enough.” His beady yellow eyes
narrow at me with suspicion. “You cannot use your sweet words to weaken me this time. I
demand vengeance for what has happened. This victory is mine.”

When he begins to face Darvik again, I reach for the leather of his belt and yank on it. I
mean, how else was I supposed to get a seven-foot plus scary alien to keep his focus on me? I
couldn’t let him hurt Darvik; even though a serious sense of Deja Vu was nudging at my
mind. Once again I was being put into the position of begging for someone else's life. Just
like I had with Li. It almost made me want to roll my eyes at the irony.



“He is not the one who took me!” I shout again, needing to be heard. “The other humans did
that! You cannot blame Darvik!”

Sin’Chila whirls back to me again with a snap of his jaws. “Darveek?… You dare to speak
this males name?” He crowds closer and I stiffen in response. My pulse thrums into my ears.

Refusing to be intimidated, I glare up at him. “They took everything from him. His ship. His
freedom. They beat him like an animal and put him in chains until--”

“I do not care what the hoomans do and do not do!” The loudness of his sudden roar makes
me flinch. “He is a heathen. Bad blood. He tried to take our female and keep her for himself.
I want his head!”

I sigh, losing the will to argue with him. “Come on, Sin’Chila, that’s not true and you know
it. He had no intention of keeping me, he was just using me to get to you.”

Although I wasn’t entirely sure, part of me believed that Darvik hadn’t known about my
mates or of the fact they were not human either. He’d probably been expecting an angry mob
of earthlings with pitchforks and torches to come to my rescue, not a clan of skilled hunters.

He hadn’t wanted me for himself. No way. He didn’t seem the sort at all. He was far too
distant and solitary a figure to want something so sentimental. And yet, a memory surges…

“Female, you are in need,” he’d purred huskily, crouching over my lying body. His chest had
brushed mine as he’d leaned down, pressing closer, inhaling deeply. His mismatched eyes
ablaze. “I will serve you.”

I shiver and shake my head to clear it. Noticing my reaction, Sin’Chila frowns. Of their own
accord, my sight trails sideways to Darvik who is as still as a statue.

Yaknaur moves into our line of vision. Puffing his chest, he growls down at the other male.
“Keep your eyes to the floor, whelp.”

Even Memnis, who is usually the voice of reason, does not interfere. He simply hovers to one
side, as observant and as quiet as a spectre.

“Sin’Chila, please,” I start again, beginning to sound like a broken record even to my own
ears. I return to him, peering up with a frantic expression. I reach for his arm again, hoping to
convey my earnestness. I lower my tone, making it almost a whisper. “He’s not a threat to
any of us. He’ll leave…” I peer around Yaknaur’s huge self to nod encouragingly to Darvik.
“Won’t you, Darvik?” I shrug. “See. There’s no harm done and no need for any further
violence.”

They all turn to Darvik, seeking his response. By their glares alone, it’s clear they’re seeking
any reason to tear him a new one. I squirm, uneasy, flashing him a meaningful look as though
I’ve suddenly become skilled in the art of telepathy.

Come on, you big, dumb idiot. Say yes. Agree and get the hell out of here.



Darvik’s white-yellow eyes bore into me, as sharp and cool as ice, yet burning with some
inner fire I could not name. He peers around at us all, measuring us all one by one. His
disdain is obvious for each of my men, but when he looks at me I see something entirely
different lurking in the depths of his scarred face.

For a long beat, he says nothing. And then his chin lifts, his shoulders broadening with a hint
of determination.

Slowly, he shakes his head.

No.

It’s there, loud and clear even though he doesn’t actually say it. He doesn’t surrender. He
doesn’t agree to our terms. He’s refusing to back down, even now when his life is hanging by

Darvik’s POV



 

There is something wrong with the female. Darvik can sense it. His senses miss nothing.
Inhaling, he gathers her scent, puzzled. She does not smell of that which is sickened or

burrowing to seek comfort. Their eyes had met many times and repeatedly she had tried to

could not fathom. He sensed no fear from her, though she made much effort to avoid him.

So then, what was it?

Leaning against the bars, he considers it further, lost in thought. The other two of her trio

As she had left, it had taken every ounce of his willpower not to reach for her. Cage or not,
the need to be close to her beat at him like a drum. Wherever she went, he wanted to follow,
and whenever she left his line of sight, his annoyance made itself known.

Give her to me.

His body ripples at the surging memory. Had he truly said such things? His weakness had
been great. But instead, where hatred had once dwelt, only a firm resolve remained now. A
change was upon him, shaking at every belief he had thought he had known. There was only
a pull now, insistent and hungry.

He could remember everything; the hooman’s cries and whimpers as her males had claimed
her, one by one. Her eyes haunted him still, dark like the reflections of a pool. Deep and
endless.

Their rutting had nearly destroyed him. Sheer envy had strangled at nearly all of his pride.
Pure torture.

He wanted her. He wanted the female for his own. He lusted for her softness. Her sleek
smooth limbs. The flaming red of her mane. He craved to covet her. To protect. To provide.
Even though he knew only too well that she belonged to another.

Grekt. He huffs, casting the enemy warrior nearby a scowl. His tail swishes across the
ground, restless. These males of hers were skilled and cunning, so he would have to tread
carefully. They meant to break him, to make him kneel.

Nej. Never. His claws clench into fists. It would never happen.

For now, the female was his focus. Patience would be his greatest ally now; a test of his will.
It was too late to turn back now. He had made his intentions clear to all of them.

The words he had spoken to the other hunters echoed through his mind, ringing more truer
than ever before, sealing his fate.

Darvik snorts and he doesn’t even try to hide his dawning amusement. The huge giant still
had the stubbornness to stand there like a king amongst peasants.

“Understand this,” Sin’Chila says coolly. “This vessel belongs to me, along with all that
resides within it. They are each under my care and keeping. The safety of my clan is my
highest priority.”

The humour in Darvik’s face fades, replaced by something almost unusual. Something raw.

Respect?

“I understand.”



Another long pause follows before Sin’Chila speaks again, harsh like a whip “Nothing here
belongs to you. Nothing.”

Darvik’s mouth splays a little, his tusks wriggling as though he wants to retort but is holding
back.

He says nothing.

Sin’Chila relaxes a notch at my side, his solid thigh pressed against mine as he holds me.

“But neither am I fool to not acknowledge when an honourable deed is done.” He speaks the
words as though it's taking every ounce of his will to do so. Begrudgingly. “I… owe you
greatly for what you have done for my female this day.”

My mouth drops open, speechless. I peer between them both, just sitting back, watching their
interaction with growing interest. Holy cow. Did he just thank Darvik? Maybe not so openly,
but it was there, loud and clear.

Darvik sneers. “I did not do this thing for you.” His gaze once again finds me.

Something in me blooms into awareness and my tummy tightens. He’d done all of this,
risked his own safety and the danger of my hunters, for me. To help me.

Unable to stop myself, I offer him a small smile of gratitude and his head inclines in a curious
manner.

“So I see,” Sin’Chila growls, drawing me harder to him. Possessively. “Very well. As a
reward, I shall give you that which you desire above all else.”

The air thickens suddenly. Darvik growl is lethal as he stalks forward a step. The sound isn’t
threatening. In fact, it's heavy with anticipation. Almost hungry. Again, he’s fixated on me.

“Yessss.” He hisses and I shudder, goosebumps spreading across my skin.

Liquid heat pools low within my body, in places where it shouldn’t.

Surely, Sin’Chila didn’t mean to--

“Your freedom.”

At that, Darvik freezes and his confusion matches that of my own.

Without missing a beat, Sin’Chila continues. “From this moment on, you are no longer our
prisoner. Now do not misunderstand. This small mercy is not one that you deserve nor should
have. It is for my female's sake that I do this. Consider this final.” He sweeps his hand out,
motioning towards the doorway. “You will go. Go now, and never return to this place again.
To do so would warrant your own demise. I have already held my kin back too many times,
but I will not do so again should you choose to ignore this last shred of leniency that I offer
you.”



“Sin’Chila?” I whisper his name, reeling at this sudden shift of events.

He’s letting Darvik go. It takes a good solid minute or two for it to sink in. He’s free to leave.
I’m elated. Relieved. Absolutely shocked. And yet also… saddened.

“Blood for blood. Life for a life. Tis a fair exchange.” Even as Sin’Chila speaks, his stare is
sharp, gleaming with an inner cunning. “This is what you wanted Vindas, no? So go. No one
will stop you.”

I hold my breath. Darvik is scowling at us both, yet behind it I sense turmoil brooding
quietly. He hesitates, considering the offer and for some reason a lump forms in my throat.
And then the truth of it hits me…

I don’t want him to go.

Before that thought can fully register, Darvik nods. Without a word, he turns and strides
towards the door with meaningful steps. Just when I think he’s about to glance back one last
time to me, he doesn’t and that’s when my eyes start to burn.

The door opens and then closes behind him. With no goodbyes and without warning, he’s
suddenly gone, leaving only me and Sin’Chila left in the med bay.

Sin’Chila lifts me further into him and I burrow into his chest, wrapping my arms around his
neck. I hide my face from him, hoping he won’t see the treacherous tears ready to spill down
my face. He croons at me gently, cupping the back of my nape which his massive hand.

It was over. It was done. Darvik was gone.

I should have been pleased. I should have been happy for him. And I was... Right?

I clutch Sin’Chila harder, fighting against myself when all I wanted to do was break down
and cry.

 



Chapter 42

I am being made to rest. First for one day, and then another after that. I don’t understand why
my mates are forcing the issue, as I have pretty much recovered already, but at the same time,
I am thankful to have this chance to just nap and reflect on things. I am not myself and we
have all noticed it. I’m lethargic and spend most of my time curled up in our nest, sleeping or
in a haze that doesn’t want to let me go. I put it down to just being tired and my body
recovering, but it’s so much more than that. Deep down, I know what the issue is, I just don’t
want to say it. Not out loud. And knowing that my males are aware is making things worse.

I sigh and nestle further into the leaves beneath me, surrounded by warmth and safety. It’s not
enough though to dispel the odd emptiness that’s aching within my chest.

I rub at my face, frustrated. I need to get a hold of myself. This isn’t like me at all. I shouldn’t
be like this. I need to get up.

Someone approaches and my bed crunches as a body lowers to kneel down next to me. A
thigh brushes my back as said person leans over to peer at my face. I look up and find that it’s
Sin’Chila, and I offer him a weak smile.

“Hi.”

His frown is troubled; an expression that seems to be a regular thing for him as of late, filling
me with guilt.

He reaches around and places his large hand against the curve of my belly. My baby.
 His
 child.

He eyes the bundle of fruits they had brought me earlier on, still untouched.

“Speak to me, Chakria,” he says. “You must eat. It is imperative for both you and the
youngling that you do so.”

“I know.” I shrug, uncertain of what to say. If I could snap out of this shroud of lowness I’d
be fine. If only it were that simple. “I don’t know what’s wrong with me.”

Sin’Chila nods with a trill as if in understanding. He observes my face closely, searching for
something. His eyes are bright and knowing.

His jaws splays, tusk clacking as he hesitates before speaking again. “
He
 is gone. There is
no need to despair for him.”

I don’t need to ask as to whom he’s talking about. It’s almost as if he can read my thoughts.
Like a ghost, Darvik is always in them, I cannot seem to exorcise him from my mind. I know
he’s gone and it’s something I should be glad about, and yet I’m not. I feel so many emotions.
An unwanted grief because he’d finally left us. Yes, I missed him, even though I had barely
even known him. And then there was the shame, the self-loathing for daring to think of



another when I had three capable and affectionate males to love me. My thoughts were
traitorous, to me and to them.

I didn’t deserve them.

I nod and turn away, praying that my face didn’t give my inner betrayal away too much.

“That’s good,” I say, quietly.

Lies. 
God, I’m such a bad liar.

Claw-tipped fingers take my chin and Sin’Chila draws me back towards him, forcing me to
meet his gaze. He bends closer, the length of one charcoal ropey lock dangling down to brush
against my cheek. The curve of his wide jaw flexes.

“He is not one of us,” he states lowly and without any anger.

Still, I swallow thickly. “I know.”

His head tilts, a curious gesture, even as his molten stare all but burns into me. Unblinking.
As though attempting to delve into my very soul.

“Then why do your eyes grieve, my female? I see pain within them, sorrow at his loss.”

I clench my lids shut and shake my head. I don’t want to talk about this. Not now. Not ever. I
want to forget about Darvik. About everything. I can’t face this feeling, these chaotic
emotions.

“I don’t--”

As soon as I start to voice my denial he cuts in fast and sure, holding my face captive even
when I try to turn away from him again.

“Do not deny it. I know you well, my mate. I see all.”

“I just pitied him, that’s all.”

He crooks an alien mottled brow. “Only pity?”

No
. It was more than that. More than mere pity. I felt a connection to Darvik because I knew
what it was like to be the outsider, the one often looked down upon. For years I’d always
been seen as the ‘invalid’, the weak one, as though I weren’t even something human but
something made of glass. I knew what it felt like to be unwanted.

I sit up then and spin to face my mate, tucking my legs beneath me. Sin’Chila gathers me to
him, shifting me until I am sat within the V of his strong lap. His searing heat is like a balm
to my senses. 

“Please, just imagine it for a moment, Sin’Chila,” I urge then, peering up at him. “Imagine a
life without your brothers. No clan. No family. No one to feel bonded to. Darvik doesn’t have



those things, and that’s what makes me sad for him.”

Sin’Chila snorts, sweeping a lock of my hair aside so, so carefully. “Your heart is too soft,
female. He is not deserving of such thoughts. He is of bad blood, you know this. A tainted
male. It is his fate to have nothing.” He trails off and a shadow casts deep within his eyes.
Thoughtful. “Although…A life without my brothers… I cannot imagine such a thing.”

I am warmed at his honesty, even a little taken aback by his emotional response. As a leader,
Sin’Chila had never been one for sentimentality. Yet here he was, outright voicing his love
for his siblings and if anything it made me admire him even more. 

My focus wanders back to Darvik. If given the chance, he’d have probably been different too.
He had a gentle side too; I knew because I’d seen it many times. But he was also primitive in
his ways, somewhat different from my own hunters, too wild and untamed to be considered
one of them.

“There are no excuses,” Sin’Chila declares firmly, his voice a low rumble. “Reason or not, he
threatened to take you from us. A great insult I could never allow to pass.”

“Well…” I hedge awkwardly, chewing at my lip. “What does it matter? He’s gone now, so…”
Again, that knot is in my throat again as my earlier low mood slowly creeps back in. What
was the point in talking about any of this? The deed was done. There no going back. I had to
accept and move on with my life.

Sin’Chila nods in curt agreement. “Indeed. So let that be the end of it.”

The end of it.
 Yes. I had to get past this and forget about Darvik. It was time to let him go. I
only hoped that, wherever he was, he was safe and thriving. 

A hand touches my shoulder and I snap to with a jolt.

“Come,” Sin’Chila says, rising to a stand and taking me with him in one swift motion. I curl
into him, cradled in his arms. I wrap mine around his neck, enjoying the closeness. “Let us go
and see the others. Perhaps some distraction will make you feel better. Yes?”

I nod, not really having anything else to say. “Ok. Sure.”

Without further ado, he walks and carries me out of the door…

 

 

As soon as I arrive on the deck, they all gather around me in welcome. Memnis chirps
happily, nuzzling in from my left. His hot breath skims my skin and I shiver. Yaknaur towers
to my right, burrowing into my throat and hissing in my scent. 

“There you are,” he says. “At last, you have decided to join us again.”



“How do you feel, 
Lartee?” 
Memnis asks. 

I offer them a small smile. “Better. The rest did me some good.”

They all croon, happy with my answer. Sin’Chila tightens his hold and in that moment
everything feels right again. Some of my anxiety drains away and leaves me. Reaching out, I
stroke each monstrously loving face in turn, giggling when Yaknaur nips at my palm lightly.
His tusks are thick and sharp even as he takes great care not to hurt me.

They argue for a time as to whom I get to sit with, each of them eager to have me close to
them or in their laps. At this point, I feel like a god damned kitten as claws and roughened
hands pet and stroke at me. I roll my eyes, smiling at their antics.

Really... These males... Despite all their scary gruffness, they really could be clingy. They
never grew bored with being around me, each of them having the patience of a saint and the
loyalty of a hound. And I loved being with them too equally as much. There wasn’t anything
I wouldn’t do for them.

I huff a laugh. “All right, that’s enough, you can put me down now.”

I wriggle from Sin’Chila’s arms and he lowers me to my feet. All three of them advance
around me, closing me within a protective circle of muscle and male strength. Unbidden,
something within me awakens and stirs, like a feline arching, craving its owner's attention. 

“I must speak with you, Brother,” Memnis says then, breaking the moment, turning serious.
“There is something I should show you. It may be of some importance.”

“Very well.” Sin’Chila glances at me as though uncertain as to whether to leave my side.

“It’s fine. You guys carry on with whatever you were doing,” I say, shooing them away with a
gesture. I point to the pile of sleeping 
Daku
 in the far corner of the room. “I’ll be over here
if you need me.”

I move away from them and they turn to face one of the nearby holographic screens. Images
sear into flickering life, rotating slowly to show the outline of our vessel. In a low tone,
Memnis speaks to Sin’Chila, pointing at something in particular, though I cannot hear what
they are discussing. 

“Are you certain?” I hear Sin’Chila ask with a slight note of what I consider to be concern.

“Yes. I have recalibrated each of our maps. For some reason, they are not picking up all of
the nearby heat signals. Some of our shields keep dropping too, though I know not why.”

“That is odd.”

“System fault, perhaps?”

“Maybe. It is difficult to say for certain.”

Memnis hums in agreement. “What do you suggest?”



Sin’Chila straightens. “Watch the main shields. If they continue to falter then we may have to
do some repairs. For now, I will go out to patrol the perimeters. I will go
 alone 
this time
.
”

“Is that wise?”

“Wise, no. Necessary, yes.” Sin’Chila adds. “I would much prefer it if the two of you
remained here. Guard the ship and our female. I will return as swiftly as I can.”

Memnis bows in acknowledgement to the unmoving order. “Yes. As you command then,
brother.”

Sin’Chila takes his helmet from a nearby wall and slips it onto his face. Air hisses as it seals
into position and the strange symbols on his wrist gauntlet flare with glowing red. As he
clenches his fist, two razor-sharp blades eject out, gleaming in the light. His spear is next,
which he snaps out, forcing it to elongate beyond his own towering height, crackling with
some unseen energy force.

“Wait for my return.”

And with that, Sin’Chila heads towards the door, purpose in his step.

As he passes me, my hand flies out, brushing against his armoured chest. “Is everything Ok?”

Sin’Chila pauses briefly at my side, leaning down to caress my cheek. “Yes, Chakria. Be at
ease. I will not be far.”

His short reply fills me with worry and he doesn’t spare me a further moment as he continues
out of the door and away from sight. I shoot Memnis a questioning look but he rotates away,
refusing to share any details.

Ugh. Typical.

Yaknaur comes over and nudges me gently towards the trio of hounds. “Go. Do not worry.
You know as well as I that nothing deters my brother once his mind is set.”

Huffing, but willing to let it slide, I make my way towards the three hulking curled up
creatures in the corner. I lower myself, huddling my human body between them. Each one
lifts their head, offering a quick yip or chin lick in welcome. Two of them rise, moving away,
leaving only the smallest one, Pup, at my side. Once comfy, I start to throw their favourite
pebble for them, laughing lightly as Prowler and Drooler scramble over each other in a
desperate bid to get to it first. Their quills quiver, their panting breaths a sign of their
excitement.

My thoughts drift, slipping back towards memories of my home planet. To say that I missed
it would be untrue. I did, and yet I didn’t. I certainly didn’t miss the people or the busy
society we humans forced ourselves to live in. But there were times when I’d think back on
the smaller pleasures of Earth; like watching TV, reading a book or even going shopping.
Those are the things that I missed. But would I be willing to trade everything I had here in
order to go back to that life? At the cost of giving up my hunters? 



No way in hell. 

This was where I belonged now. In hindsight, regardless of all the hassle and horror I’d gone
through since coming here, I was glad that it had happened. I was happy here. 

I lob the rock again and one of the hounds rushes forward and nabs it. Prowler circles as if to
return to me, only to pause midway. He drops his prize and sniffs at the air suddenly, a low
growl forming in his throat.

Weird...

I frown and peer around the room, my skin prickling as if expecting to find something out of
place, a reason for his sudden shift in demeanour.

My hound prowls towards the entrance, which is still sitting wide open. His snarls grow,
becoming feral. Lips curl and teeth flash, snapping at the air. No… At 
something
 beyond the
doorway.

“What’s wrong, boy?” I talk to him, but the beast doesn’t respond or move away. “What is
it?”

My brows deepen. Memnis, hearing the commotion, looks over his shoulder with a
questioning lift of his brow.

I shrug. “I don’t know what’s wrong with him. He’s not usually like this.” I return my focus
to the hound and rise to my feet. I try to peer into the shadows, confused. 

What is he looking at?

“Here, boy,” I call. “That’s enough. Come.” I pat at my leg, hoping he’d return to my side,
but he doesn’t.

If anything, his venomous growls increase even more. His beading eyes are trained on
something and growing wilder with each passing second. Kinda freaking me out, to be
honest. 

Without warning, Prowler lunges forwards, skidding out into the corridor, howling loudly
like something possessed.

With a yelp of surprise, I dash out to follow him. My bare feet clang across the darkened steel
floor as I part-run-part-stumble my way out into the corridor too. I draw abruptly still as I
find that my lizard-like friend has stopped halfway down and is going nuts, barking savagely
at something near the… um, ceiling?

What the...

I inch forward, on edge, cautious, only to then freeze…

Alarm surges as something shimmers there, hidden amongst the piping and within the
darkness where the dim glow of the lights don’t quite cover. The metal wall ripples there like



the surface of a pond; an eerily familiar sight. The silvery outline takes shape and steadily I
begin to realise what it is.

A figure.

Something is there, or rather someone. Clinging to the wall like a spider. Inhuman and huge.

The haze shifts and a solid body takes its place revealing the herculean shape of a hunter.
Male, but not one of mine... And not Darvik either.

A deadly array of clicks echo and fear rips down my back. A pair of blood-red eyes flash into
being from the gloom, staring right at me. I make out the trademark long thick locks and
heavily muscled body, a splitting grin of tusks that seem to sneer at me.

Oh... shit.

I am given no warning, no time to even register what is about to happen. With a roar, the
unknown Yautja lunges down from his height, coming straight for me. I spin, emitting a shrill
shriek of terror, my heart flying into my throat.

“Memnis! Yaknaur!” I scream, the sound echoing. “Intruder!”

And then I start to run.



Chapter 43

 

Sin’Chila’s POV

 

Sin’Chila scans the horizon, searching its vast and endless length for something, though he
knew not what. The trees creak, their vibrant leaves swaying in a breeze. Shadows cast,
hiding him from all and any who may wish to seek him out. He draws in a hiss, inhaling the
scents of his homeland. He tastes each one, recognising every detail, every creature that
might have passed through the area. Crouched, he shifts position within the tree. Alert. His
helmet is heavy, blocking some of his natural sight, but it allows him the advantage of
tracking the heat signals of any potential prey nearby. Creatures of the jungle still, some of
them scuttling away in the foliage as if aware of his presence, anticipating a predator amongst
them. With his own senses heightened, a sensation licks at his back; a feeling as old as time
itself, warning him of nearby danger. His instincts never lied.

But what was it? He could not rightly tell. Memnis’ earlier warnings still rang through his
mind. Some strange happenings had been occurring with their ship. Systems were not
operating as they should be, yet having checked each one he could find any damages or
reasons to cause this.

Odd. 
Very odd indeed.

For what seemed like an age, he continues to watch the wilderness. Wary. Unable to shake
the notion that something was amiss. His gaze scores the ground below him, seeing nothing.
In the distance, their ship sits like a temple upon a crest. Shining and brilliant. His home.

There is no movement and he hears nothing save for the subtly controlled hisses of his
breath.

All is quiet. All is still. Which gives him only little comfort.

Restless, he leaps from one trunk to another, his footing deft and stealthy, like a true hunter of
his kind. He circles the perimeter of his territory, scouting for any signs of disturbance. The
sun rises in the sky, creeping higher and higher as time passes. Before he knows it, its
brightness is starting to dip again a sure sign that night would soon be upon them.

Uncertain, he leaps down with a thud. The ground shakes with the impact and he emits a low
trill, continuously peering around. Expectant.

It is nothing
. He reasons to himself.
 Tis but a trick of your mind. All is well.  

Huffing, he begins to head for the ramp, intending the return to the others. He makes it a few
steps and then pauses, a musky scent coming at him from nowhere. Unfamiliar.



He inhales deep and slowly turns his head, looking down. There, slightly concealed, he spots
an unnatural groove in the terrain, barely visible. Moving closer, he crouches low, a frown
forming.

A footprint.

He reaches out, brushing his fingers into its shape, feeling it for every nuance and
imperfection. It is of a formidable size. Male, highly likely. Clawed and webbed, much like
his own. Definitely Yautja. Though not made by that of his kin.

The Vindas? he shakes his head.
 No
. That hunter should have moved on and been long gone
by now. Surely the fool would not tempt fate again. Not when--

A sound comes. Harsh and sharp, familiar in its clarity. A shriek…

Like that of a 
hooman. 

His hooman.

His head jerks up. Dread and fury lace through his being, ripping away his logic. The cry
comes again and it's enough to force him to his feet. Gripping his spear, Sin’Chila races for
the ship, his legs burning with all the speed he can muster.

 

 

I run like the devil himself his chasing me. Heavy footsteps pound behind me, gaining fast
and another scream rips through my throat. Sheer fear has me in its hold as adrenaline drives
me into moving faster.

The hunter behind me is right at my back as I hurtle down the hall, aiming for the deck. But
I’m not fast enough. I can feel his breath on my nape. He so, so close. I’m not going to make
it.

“Memnis!”

As if my desperate cry has conjured them into being, Memnis and Yaknaur appear at the
other end of the corridor. They race towards me, eyes ablaze and roaring as they quickly
notice the male chasing me down.

“
Lartee
!”

Just when I think Yakanur is about to barrel right into me, he launches up and twists in mid-
air, flying over my person to land on my other side, slamming straight down onto the
intruder. I don’t stop to look. Frantic, I head straight for Memnis, diving into his waiting arms
like a frightened child.



“Oh my god! Oh my god!” I’m blinded by panic. “What’s happening? Who the fuck is 
that
!”

Without answering, Memnis squeezes me once before placing me behind him, pressing me
back towards the wall.

“Stay here!”

And with that, he rushes in after his brother and into the fight. In fearful awe, I watch as the
two of them grapple against the larger male, trying to use sheer brute strength to bring him
down to the ground. The other male is far too quick for them. Swiftly, he dodges each blow,
biting and slashing out with claws and tusks, his red eyes menacing and burning with only
one purpose.

To kill. 

Arm flying out, Memnis shoots out an array of spiked blades from his gauntlet. They whizz
through the air, only one of them making their mark. The male snarls, rearing back. Gripping
the knife, the hunter tears it free from his shoulder, not even flinching at the pain, before
sneering darkly at my mate.

“Weakling.” His voice rumbles like an animal, so deep that my translator takes a while to
convert what he’s saying.

They all surge at one another once more, battling it out, muscle vs muscle. Strength vs
strength.

I can’t breathe. I can’t even think. I lean back further into the wall as the world spins. My
vision starts to swim. I’m hyperventilating.

Oh god, please don’t pass out.
 I will to myself. 
Not now
.

The other male is taking on mine with frightening ease. He tosses Memnis aside, before
sending out a kick to Yaknaur’s midriff. Winded, he stumbles back. No sooner had he fallen,
both he and his brother are back on their feet, rushing at their enemy with vigour.

Seemingly, out of nowhere, another figure emerges, sprinting his way towards us and directly
into combat.

“Sin’Chila.” I sigh his name as I recognise him.

The walls and floor vibrate under their assault. Snarls and howls of pain ricochet around me,
growing in crescendo, matching the raging rush of my pulse within my ears.

I shriek when Memnis is grabbed. Held by his throat, the intruder lifts him high, holding him
there for a beat before slamming him down the ground.

What do I do? What could I do? There wasn’t any way that I could help them; I wasn’t a
Yautja like them. With any attempt to do so I knew I’d probably get crushed in the chaos. I



feel so useless at this moment. My mates are fighting for our safety and I’m just standing
there, quietly having a mini breakdown.

“Yakanur! Get up!” I shout when I realise he’s down again and having difficulty getting back
to his feet. He staggers, shaking his head and it’s then that I see the luminous blood seeping
down the side of his face.

Sin’Chila twirls his spear with eerie grace and skill, swinging it out hard into the unknown
hunter's chest. The strike is so hard that the male is ripped from his feet and sent flying down
the hall…

Exactly towards my location.

I yelp and dive away at the last minute as his herculean body slams into the wall and the
metal dents under the impact. Hot air stirs around me. I start to sidestep, hoping the hunter
won’t realise I’m right there next to him.

He’s too close. Far too close for comfort.

I hear hurried footsteps approach.

“Chakria!” Sin’Chila calls. “Move away! Now!”

Spitting blood, the hunter starts to rise, bracing his palms to the floor, trying to lift himself to
a stand. I whisper a gasp and suddenly his head lifts and a set of glowing crimson eyes meet
mine. His flaring grin is manic as a shard fear impales me, stealing my breath in an instant.
I’m pretty much doomed at this point. I know it. My trio rushes to reach me first but it is too
late...

I try to bolt away and the Yautja launches at me, so fast, like a train at full speed. I don’t even
have the chance to make a sound. Claws snag into my hair and a scorching pain rips down
my back. Legs flailing, I finally scream as the male drags me up, practically lifting me with
ease from my feet to dangle in his hold.

“Let me go!”

Tears pour down my face at the agony. I kick out, struggling wildly, desperate to be free.

The hunter turns, holding me out as if I’m nothing more than a squirming rabbit for the others
to see.

“One more step… and the female dies.”

I’m absolutely terrified and the pain is unbearable. My wide eyes find my mates from across
the distance. They’ve each stopped in their advance, uncertain of how to proceed, even as
their sunken eyes burn with murderous rage.

My captor trills in victory, his laughter dark.

“Warriors, you have lost. This ship is now mine. I claim it!”



My skeleton feels like it's being ripped out from the fleshy shell of my body. My head is
pounding. All of my senses are focused on my scalp which feels like it’s being set on fire.
There’s so much strain being placed on my spine and neck that I start to choke.

“Release her,” Sin’Chila growls. So deep. So dangerously.

My captor hisses a sneer and gives me an agonising shake as though I were nothing more
than a child’s doll. “
Nej
. She will perish, as will the rest of you. Accept your defeat now and
give me the--

Chapter 44

They tolerate him; that’s all I can really say from this point on. Darvik soon becomes like a
mosquito amongst us; hard to get rid of, always there, hanging around even when no one

though I’d never admit it aloud. But the others? No. It was obvious they disliked him.

A begrudging kind of mutual peace had settled between my hunters and Darvik, although I

if he were the plague, sometimes even pretending he wasn’t there at all. I often found

accepting Darvik but neither discarding him or forcing him to go. Which I didn’t fully

deemed as intruding within his territory. Yet he didn’t. And now and then he would look to
me, a secretive gleam across his face. Knowing. Questioning. As though waiting for me to do
or say something.

I kept to myself, saying nothing, as this rivalry between these males were beyond my calibre.
I didn’t dare say what I really thought or felt for fear of causing offence or disrupting the tiny
nugget of calm that had morphed within our home. Instead, the males around me adjusted
and even Darvik himself learned to respect the boundaries of my mates. They did not provide
for him; he hunted for his own kills. He was restricted to specific parts of the ship,
particularly kept away from our private nesting quarters. He had no right to demand anything.
He kept to his cell but was free to leave whenever he pleased; he wasn’t a prisoner here. And

all with respect and a small hint of cunning that kept us all on our toes.

disagreeing bulls. Yaknaur, forever being the antagoniser, continued to push at the other

Yaknaur snorts, throwing him a quick glare before straightening and twirling his spear,

Men. I tut to myself and shake my head.

Chapter 45

The following day…

----

--

“Come… Hunt with us.”

I almost choke on the berries I’m eating when I heard Sin’Chila say those words to Darvik.
Sat in my corner, I swivel myself around on my ass to face them with an expression of
outright shock.

Did I just hear him correctly? Is my baby addled brain playing tricks on me? Surely I was
imagining things. Did he really just ask him to go hunting with them? Holy macaroni.

Yaknaur advances, striding away from his position at a nearby station. His face mirrors mine,
only there is a muted fury there as well. His eyes blaze brightly, flickering between the figure
of his brother and the huge male lounging within his cell.

Everyone in the room shifts. Awkward and unsettled. Memnis casts me a wary look and I can
tell he’s thinking the exact same thing as I am. I shrug at him subtly in an ‘I have no idea
what is happening’ kind of way. The tension throughout the deck is like a living breathing
thing, weaving around us all, straining and tight, causing a ripple of unease to creep down my
back.

Sin’Chila’s expression gives nothing away, his attention fixed upon Darvik as he stands over
him, shrouding him within the length of his massive shadow.

“Well?” His tone is patient, incredibly cool.

“You wish for me to join you?” Darvik asks with some uncertainty.

“Is that wise?” I say, even though I’m not entirely sure who I am speaking to. All of them,
maybe.

I can’t help my unease. Sin’Chila, Yaknaur and Darvik, hunting together? Oh, hell no. The
idea alone made me want to laugh and cry at the same time. Was Sin’Chila being serious? It
was the equivalent of inviting three angry, hungry lions to a children’s tea party and not
expecting there to be chaos or casualties. Sheer madness.

“Brother, no.” Yaknaur glides to his kins side. Insistent. “He is not one of us! How can you
welcome him into our group? He will be of no use and will only get in our way, we do not
need him. He should--”



Sin’Chila lifts an arm, motioning for silence. Yaknaur stills, bowing his head in respect.
Reticent.

“Am I the leader of this clan or am I not?” Sin’Chila asks with a firm tone before returning to

Memnis will stay here with our female. But you…” He points down to Darvik. “I will have

your place.”

I sit there, observing the exchange warily, fiddling with the edge of my makeshift tunic. For a

“Very well. I shall follow you.”

overwhelming urge to hug him.
